**Andre Kirk Agassi** ([/ˈæɡəsi/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*AG-ə-see*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key");[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-3)[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-4) born April 29, 1970) is an American former [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_players "List of ATP number 1 ranked singles players") [tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis "Tennis") player.[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bio-5) He is an eight-time [major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Slam_\(tennis\)#Tournaments "Grand Slam (tennis)") champion, an [Olympic gold medalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_1996_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_singles "Tennis at the 1996 Summer Olympics – Men's singles"), and a runner-up in seven other majors. Agassi is the second of five men in the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_tennis#Open_Era "History of tennis") to achieve the [Career Grand Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Grand_Slam_men%27s_singles_champions#Career_Grand_Slam "List of Grand Slam men's singles champions").[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-6)[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-7)[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-8)[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-9) He is also the first man to complete both the [Career Golden Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Grand_Slam_men%27s_singles_champions#Career_Golden_Slam "List of Grand Slam men's singles champions")[\[a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-10) and the [Career Super Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Grand_Slam_men%27s_singles_champions#Career_Super_Slam "List of Grand Slam men's singles champions")[\[b\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-11), achieving this feat in 1999.[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SI-12)

Agassi was the first man to win all four singles majors on three different surfaces ([hard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcourt "Hardcourt"), [clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay_court "Clay court") and [grass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grass_court "Grass court")), and remains the most recent American man to win the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Open "French Open") (in 1999)[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-13) and the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Open "Australian Open") (in [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Australian_Open "2003 Australian Open")).[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-14) He also won 17 [Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour_Masters_1000 "ATP Tour Masters 1000") titles and was part of the winning [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup "Davis Cup") teams in 1990, 1992 and 1995.[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tennis-15) Agassi reached the world No. 1 ranking for the first time in 1995, but was troubled by personal issues during the mid-to-late 1990s and sank to No. 141 in 1997, prompting many to believe that his career was over.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) Agassi returned to No. 1 in 1999 and enjoyed the most successful run of his career over the next four years. During his 20-plus year tour career, Agassi was known as "The Punisher".[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-17)[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-18)[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-19)[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-20) After suffering from [sciatica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciatica "Sciatica") caused by two bulging discs in his back, a [spondylolisthesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spondylolisthesis "Spondylolisthesis") ([vertebral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertebra "Vertebra") displacement) and a [bone spur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bone_spur "Bone spur") that interfered with the [nerve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nerve "Nerve"), Agassi retired from professional tennis after the [2006 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_singles "2006 US Open – Men's singles").

He is the founder of the Andre Agassi Charitable Foundation,[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-21) which has raised over $60 million for at-risk children in Southern Nevada.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-22) In 2001, the Foundation opened the Andre Agassi College Preparatory Academy in Las Vegas, a K–12 public charter school for at-risk children.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-23) He has been married to fellow tennis player [Steffi Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steffi_Graf "Steffi Graf") since 2001.[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-24) Agassi was named [Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laver_Cup "Laver Cup") captain of Team World in 2024, beginning with the 2025 annual competition, succeeding [John McEnroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McEnroe "John McEnroe").[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-25)

## Early life

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=1 "Edit section: Early life")]

Andre Agassi was born in Las Vegas, Nevada, to [Emmanuel "Mike" Agassi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Agassi "Emmanuel Agassi") (Aghassian), a former Olympic boxer from Iran,[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-26) and American Elizabeth "Betty" Agassi (née Dudley).[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bio-5)[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-27) His father is of [Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Armenians "Iranian Armenians") and [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_people "Assyrian people") heritage and was a casino worker and former [amateur boxer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amateur_boxing "Amateur boxing").[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgassiCobelloWelsh20041,_12%E2%80%9314-28)[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-persianbio-29)[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-zindamagazine.com-30)[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-peoplebio-31)[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-32)[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-The_man_behind_Andre-33) His parents married in 1959 after dating for two months, then moved from [Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago "Chicago") to Las Vegas.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgassiCobelloWelsh200462%E2%80%9364,_68-34) He has three older siblings: Rita (who was married to former number one tennis player [Pancho Gonzales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pancho_Gonzales "Pancho Gonzales")), Philip and Tami.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-35) Andre was given the middle name Kirk after [Kirk Kerkorian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirk_Kerkorian "Kirk Kerkorian"), an Armenian-American businessman. Emmanuel Agassi, then a waiter at [Tropicana Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropicana_Las_Vegas "Tropicana Las Vegas"), had met his employer Kerkorian in 1963 and they became friends.[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgassi201047-36)[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-37)

At the age of 12, Agassi and his good friend and doubles partner, Roddy Parks, won the 1982 National Indoor Boys 14s Doubles Championship in Chicago.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-38) Agassi describes memorable experiences and juvenile pranks with Roddy in his book *Open*.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgassi201062,_63-39)

When he was 13, Agassi was sent to [Nick Bollettieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Bollettieri "Nick Bollettieri")'s Tennis Academy in Florida.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) He was meant to stay for only three months, because that was all his father could afford. After thirty minutes of watching Agassi play, Bollettieri, deeply impressed by his talent, called Mike and said: "Take your check back. He's here for free."[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-lxbpdn-40) Agassi then dropped out of school in the ninth grade to pursue a full-time tennis career.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-41)

## Professional career

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=2 "Edit section: Professional career")]

### 1986–1993: Breakthrough and the first major title

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=3 "Edit section: 1986–1993: Breakthrough and the first major title")]

Agassi turned professional at the age of 16 and competed in his first tournament at [La Quinta, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Quinta,_California "La Quinta, California"). He won his first match against [John Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Austin_\(tennis\) "John Austin (tennis)"), but then lost his second match to [Mats Wilander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mats_Wilander "Mats Wilander"). By the end of 1986, Agassi was ranked No. 91.[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Tennis28-42) He won his first top-level singles title in 1987 at the [Sul American Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Itaparica "ATP Itaparica") in [Itaparica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itaparica "Itaparica")[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) and ended the year ranked No. 25.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) He won six additional tournaments in 1988 (Memphis, [U.S. Men's Clay Court Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_U.S._Men%27s_Clay_Court_Championships "1988 U.S. Men's Clay Court Championships"), [Forest Hills WCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Championship_Tennis#WCT_1988 "World Championship Tennis"), Stuttgart Outdoor, [Volvo International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Volvo_International "1988 Volvo International") and [Livingston Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Livingston_Open "1988 Livingston Open")),[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) and, by December of that year, he had surpassed US$1 million in career prize money after playing in just 43 tournaments—the fastest anyone in history had reached that level.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-43)[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-44) During 1988, he also set the open-era record for most consecutive victories by a male teenager (a record that stood for 17 years until [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal") broke it in 2005).[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-45) His year-end ranking was No. 3, behind second-ranked [Ivan Lendl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Lendl "Ivan Lendl") and top-ranked [Mats Wilander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mats_Wilander "Mats Wilander"). Both the [Association of Tennis Professionals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals "Association of Tennis Professionals") and *Tennis* magazine named Agassi the Most Improved Player of the Year for 1988.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16)

In addition to not playing the Australian Open (which later became his best Grand Slam event) for the first eight years of his career, Agassi chose not to play at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Championships,_Wimbledon "The Championships, Wimbledon") from 1988 through 1990 (although he first played there in 1987, only to lose in the first round to [Henri Leconte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henri_Leconte "Henri Leconte")) and publicly stated that he did not wish to play there because of the event's traditionalism, particularly its "predominantly white" dress code to which players at the event are required to conform.[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-46)[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-47)

Strong performances on the tour meant that Agassi was quickly tipped as a future Grand Slam champion. While still a teenager, he reached the semi-finals of both the French Open and the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Open_\(tennis\) "US Open (tennis)") in 1988 and made the US Open semi-finals in 1989. He began the 1990s with a series of near-misses. He reached his first Grand Slam final in 1990 at the French Open, where he was favored before losing in four sets to [Andrés Gómez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_G%C3%B3mez "Andrés Gómez"), which he later attributed in his book to worrying about his wig falling off during the match.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-48) He reached his second Grand Slam final of the year at the US Open, defeating defending champion [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker "Boris Becker") in the semi-finals. His opponent in the final was [Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras"); a year earlier, Agassi had crushed Sampras, after which time he told his coach that he felt bad for Sampras because he was never going to make it as a pro. Agassi lost the US Open final to Sampras in three sets.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) The [Agassi-Sampras rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agassi-Sampras_rivalry "Agassi-Sampras rivalry") became the biggest one in tennis over the rest of the decade. Agassi ended 1990 on a high note as he helped the United States win its first [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup "Davis Cup") in 8 years on home soil against Australia (3–2) and won his only [Tennis Masters Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Cup "Tennis Masters Cup"), beating reigning Wimbledon champion [Stefan Edberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Edberg "Stefan Edberg") in the final.

In 1991, Agassi reached his second consecutive French Open final, where he faced fellow Bollettieri Academy alumnus [Jim Courier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Courier "Jim Courier"). Courier emerged the victor in a five-set final. The Las Vegan was a set and 3–1 up when came the rain. The rain delay proved to be a confidence builder for Courier. Agassi decided to play at Wimbledon in 1991, leading to weeks of speculation in the media about the clothes he would wear. He eventually emerged for the first round in a completely white outfit.[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-49) He reached the quarterfinals on that occasion, losing in five sets to [David Wheaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Wheaton "David Wheaton").

Agassi's Grand Slam tournament breakthrough came at Wimbledon, not at the French Open or the US Open, where he had previously enjoyed success. In 1992, he defeated [Goran Ivanišević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goran_Ivani%C5%A1evi%C4%87 "Goran Ivanišević") in a five-set final.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16)[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-50) Along the way, Agassi overcame two former Wimbledon champions: [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker "Boris Becker") and [John McEnroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McEnroe "John McEnroe"). No other baseliner would triumph at Wimbledon until [Lleyton Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lleyton_Hewitt "Lleyton Hewitt") ten years later. Agassi was named the [BBC Overseas Sports Personality of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year_Overseas_Personality "BBC Sports Personality of the Year Overseas Personality") in 1992. Agassi once again played on the United States' [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup "Davis Cup") winning team in 1992. It was their second Davis cup title in three years. Agassi famously played the game wearing [Oakley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakley,_Inc. "Oakley, Inc.") brand sunglasses, and a photo of him from the day appeared on the cover of [*Tennis* magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_\(magazine\) "Tennis (magazine)"). In his memoir, he wrote that he was covering up bloodshot eyes from a [hangover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hangover "Hangover") and claimed that the founder of Oakley, [Jim Jannard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Jannard "Jim Jannard"), had sent him a [Dodge Viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodge_Viper "Dodge Viper") to thank him for the inadvertent publicity.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-51)

In 1993, Agassi won the only doubles title of his career, at the [Cincinnati Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Masters "Cincinnati Masters"), partnered with [Petr Korda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Korda "Petr Korda"). He missed much of the early part of that year due to injuries. Although he made the quarterfinals in his Wimbledon title defense, he lost to eventual champion and No. 1 Pete Sampras in five sets. Agassi lost in the first round at the US Open to [Thomas Enqvist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Enqvist "Thomas Enqvist") and required wrist surgery late in the year.

### 1994–1997: Rise to the top, Olympic Gold and the fall

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=4 "Edit section: 1994–1997: Rise to the top, Olympic Gold and the fall")]

With new coach [Brad Gilbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Gilbert "Brad Gilbert") on board, Agassi began to employ more of a tactical, consistent approach, which fueled his resurgence. He started slowly in 1994, losing in the first week at the French Open and Wimbledon, although he did receive a much-needed confidence boost after defeating [Mark Petchey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Petchey "Mark Petchey") at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\) "Miami Open (tennis)") in March. Nevertheless, he emerged during the hard-court season, winning the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Masters "Canada Masters"). His comeback culminated at the [1994 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_US_Open_\(tennis\) "1994 US Open (tennis)") with a five-set fourth-round victory against [Michael Chang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Chang "Michael Chang"). He then became the first man to capture the US Open as an [unseeded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seed_\(tennis\) "Seed (tennis)") player, beating [Michael Stich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Stich "Michael Stich") in the final.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) Along the way, he beat 5 seeded players.

In 1995, Agassi shaved his balding head, breaking with his old "image is everything" style. He competed in the [1995 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Australian_Open "1995 Australian Open") (his first appearance at the event) and won, beating defending champion Sampras in a four-set final.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) Agassi and Sampras met in five tournament finals in 1995, all on [hardcourt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcourt "Hardcourt"), with Agassi winning three. Agassi won three Masters Series events in 1995 ([Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Masters "Cincinnati Masters"), [Key Biscayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Masters "Miami Masters"), and the Canadian Open) and seven titles total.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) He compiled a career-best 26-match winning streak during the summer hard-court circuit, with the last victory being in an intense late-night four-set semi-final of the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_US_Open_\(tennis\) "1995 US Open (tennis)") against [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker "Boris Becker"). The streak ended the next day when Agassi lost the final to Sampras. Agassi admitted this loss, which gave Sampras a 9–8 lead in their head-to-head meetings, took two years for him to get over mentally.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tennis-buzz1995-52)

Agassi reached the [world No. 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ATP_number_1_ranked_singles_tennis_players "List of ATP number 1 ranked singles tennis players") ranking for the first time in April 1995. He held that ranking until November, for a total of 30 weeks. Agassi skipped most of the fall indoor season which allowed Sampras to surpass him and finish ranked No. 1 at the year-end ranking. In terms of win–loss record, 1995 was Agassi's best year. He won 73 and lost 9 matches, and was also once again a key player on the United States' [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup "Davis Cup") winning team—the third and final Davis Cup title of his career.

1996 was a less successful year for Agassi, as he failed to reach any Grand Slam final. He suffered two early-round losses to [Chris Woodruff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Woodruff "Chris Woodruff") and [Doug Flach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_Flach "Doug Flach") at the French Open and Wimbledon, respectively, and lost to Chang in straight sets in the Australian and US Open semi-finals. At the time, Agassi blamed the Australian Open loss on the windy conditions, but later said in his biography that he had lost the match on purpose, as he did not want to play Boris Becker, whom he would have faced in that final. The high point for Agassi was winning the men's singles gold medal at the [Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Summer_Olympics "1996 Summer Olympics") in [Atlanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta "Atlanta"), beating [Sergi Bruguera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergi_Bruguera "Sergi Bruguera") of Spain in the final.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) Agassi also successfully defended his singles titles in Cincinnati and Key Biscayne.

1997 was the low point of Agassi's career. His wrist injury resurfaced, and he played only 24 matches during the year. He later confessed that he used [crystal methamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methamphetamine "Methamphetamine") during that time, allegedly on the urging of a friend.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-sports.espn.go.com-53) He failed an ATP drug test, but wrote a letter claiming the same friend had spiked a drink. The ATP dropped the failed drug test as a warning. In his autobiography, Agassi admitted that the letter was a lie.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-54) He quit the drug soon after. At this time Agassi was also in a failing marriage with actress, model, and socialite [Brooke Shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_Shields "Brooke Shields") and had lost interest in the game.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-55) He won no top-level titles, and his ranking sank to No. 141 on November 10, 1997, prompting many to believe that his run as one of the sport's premier competitors was over and he would never again win any significant tournaments.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16)

### 1998–2003: Return to glory and Career Super Slam

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=5 "Edit section: 1998–2003: Return to glory and Career Super Slam")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Andre_Agassi_1999.jpg/220px-Andre_Agassi_1999.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Andre_Agassi_1999.jpg)

Agassi in 1999

In 1998, Agassi began a rigorous conditioning program and worked his way back up the rankings by playing in Challenger Series tournaments, a circuit for pro players ranked outside the world's top 50. After returning to top physical and mental shape, Agassi recorded the most successful period of his tennis career and also played classic matches in that period against [Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras") and [Patrick Rafter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Rafter "Patrick Rafter").

In 1998, Agassi won five titles and leapt from No. 110 to No. 6, the highest jump into the top 10 made by any player during a calendar year.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-atpbio-56) At Wimbledon, he had an early loss in the second round to [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas "Tommy Haas"). He won five titles in ten finals and was runner-up at the [Masters Series tournament in Key Biscayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Masters "Miami Masters"), losing to [Marcelo Ríos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_R%C3%ADos "Marcelo Ríos"), who became No. 1 as a result. At the year end he was awarded the ATP Most Improved Player of the Year for the second time in his career (the first being 10 years earlier in 1988).

Agassi entered the history books in 1999 when he came back from two sets to love down to beat [Andrei Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Medvedev_\(tennis\) "Andrei Medvedev (tennis)") in a five-set French Open final, becoming, at the time, only the fifth male player (joining [Rod Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver "Rod Laver"), [Fred Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Perry "Fred Perry"), [Roy Emerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Emerson "Roy Emerson") and [Don Budge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Budge "Don Budge")—these have since been joined by [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer"), [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal"), and [Novak Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic")) to win all four Grand Slam singles titles during his career. Only Laver, Agassi, Federer, Nadal and Djokovic have achieved this feat during the [Open Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Era "Open Era"). This win also made him the first (of only four, the next being Federer, Nadal and Djokovic respectively) male player in history to have won all four Grand Slam titles on three different surfaces (clay, grass and hard courts). Agassi also became the first male player to win the [Career Super Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Career_Super_Slam "Career Super Slam"), consisting of all four Grand Slam tournaments plus an Olympic gold medal in singles and a [Year-end championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_World_Tour_Finals "ATP World Tour Finals").[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SI-12)

Agassi followed his 1999 French Open victory by reaching the Wimbledon final, where he lost to Sampras in straight sets.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) He rebounded from his Wimbledon defeat by winning the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_US_Open_\(tennis\) "1999 US Open (tennis)"), beating [Todd Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Martin "Todd Martin") in five sets (rallying from a two sets to one deficit) in the final. Overall during the year Agassi won 5 titles including two majors and the ATP Masters Series in Paris, where he beat [Marat Safin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marat_Safin "Marat Safin"). Agassi ended 1999 as the No. 1, ending Sampras's record of six consecutive year-ending top rankings (1993–98).[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) This was the only time Agassi ended the year at No. 1. Agassi was runner-up to Sampras at the year-end [Tennis Masters Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Tennis_Masters_Cup "1999 Tennis Masters Cup") losing 1–6, 5–7, 4–6 despite beating Sampras in the round-robin 6–2, 6–2.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-57)

He began the next year 2000 by capturing his second Australian Open title, beating Sampras in a five-set semi-final and [Yevgeny Kafelnikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yevgeny_Kafelnikov "Yevgeny Kafelnikov") in a four-set final.[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) He was the first male player to have reached four consecutive Grand Slam finals since [Rod Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver "Rod Laver") achieved the Grand Slam in 1969.[\[c\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-58) At the time, Agassi was also only the fourth player since Laver to be the reigning champion of three of four Grand Slam events, missing only the Wimbledon title.[\[d\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-59). 2000 also saw Agassi reach the semi-finals at Wimbledon, where he lost in five sets to Rafter in a match considered by many to be one of the best ever at Wimbledon.[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-60) At the inaugural [Tennis Masters Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Tennis_Masters_Cup "2000 Tennis Masters Cup") in Lisbon, Agassi reached the final after defeating Marat Safin in the semi-finals to end the Russian's hopes to become the youngest No. 1 in the history of tennis. Agassi then lost to [Gustavo Kuerten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustavo_Kuerten "Gustavo Kuerten") in the final, allowing Kuerten to be crowned year-end No. 1.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-61)

Agassi opened 2001 by successfully defending his Australian Open title with a straight-sets final win over [Arnaud Clément](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnaud_Cl%C3%A9ment "Arnaud Clément").[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-greatath-16) En route, he beat a cramping Rafter in five sets in front of a sell-out crowd in what turned out to be the Aussie's last Australian Open. At Wimbledon, they met again in the semi-finals, where Agassi lost another close match to Rafter, 8–6 in the fifth set. In the quarterfinals at the US Open, Agassi lost a 3-hour, 33 minute epic match[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-62) with Sampras, 7–6, 6–7, 6–7, 6–7,[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-63) with no breaks of serve during the 52-game match. Despite the setback, Agassi finished 2001 ranked No. 3, becoming the only male tennis player to finish a year ranked in the top 3 in three different decades.[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-64)[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-atpbio-56)

2002 opened with disappointment for Agassi, as injury forced him to skip the Australian Open, where he was a two-time defending champion.[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-65) Agassi recovered from the injury and later that year defended his [Key Biscayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Key_Biscayne "Key Biscayne") title beating then rising Roger Federer in a four-set final. At the US Open, Agassi overcame No.1 ranked and defending champion [Lleyton Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lleyton_Hewitt "Lleyton Hewitt") in the semi-finals.[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-66) This led to what turned out to be the last duel between Agassi and Sampras in final of the US Open, which Sampras won in four sets and left Sampras with a 20–14 edge in their 34 career meetings. The match was the last of Sampras's career. Agassi's US Open finish, along with his Masters Series victories in Key Biscayne, [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rome_Masters "Rome Masters") and [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Open_\(tennis\) "Madrid Open (tennis)"), helped him finish 2002 as the oldest year-end No. 2 at 32 years and 8 months.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-atpbio-56)

In 2003, Agassi won the eighth (and final) Grand Slam title of his career at the Australian Open, where he beat [Rainer Schüttler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainer_Sch%C3%BCttler "Rainer Schüttler") in straight sets in the final.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-67)

On April 28, 2003, he recaptured the No. 1 ranking to become the oldest top-ranked male player since the ATP rankings began at 33 years and 13 days. The record was later surpassed by [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") in 2018. He had held the No. 1 ranking for two weeks, when [Lleyton Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lleyton_Hewitt "Lleyton Hewitt") took it back on May 12, 2003. Agassi then recaptured the No. 1 ranking once again on June 16, 2003, which he held for 12 weeks until September 7, 2003. There he managed to reach the US Open semi-finals, where he lost to [Juan Carlos Ferrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Carlos_Ferrero "Juan Carlos Ferrero"), surrendering his No. 1 ranking to him. During his career, Agassi held the ranking for a total of 101 weeks. Agassi's ranking slipped when injuries forced him to withdraw from a number of events. At the year-end Tennis Masters Cup, Agassi lost in the final to Federer, his third time to finish as runner-up in the event after losses in 1999 and 2000, and finished the year ranked No. 4.[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-68) At age 33, he had been one of the oldest players to rank in the top 5 since Connors, at age 35, was No. 4 in 1987.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-atpbio-56)

### 2004–2006: Final years

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=6 "Edit section: 2004–2006: Final years")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Andre_Agassi_Indian_Wells_2006.jpg/220px-Andre_Agassi_Indian_Wells_2006.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Andre_Agassi_Indian_Wells_2006.jpg)

Agassi in 2006

In 2004, Agassi began the year with a five-set loss in the semi-finals of the Australian Open to [Marat Safin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marat_Safin "Marat Safin"); the loss ended Agassi's 26-match winning streak at the event. He won the [Masters series event in Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Masters "Cincinnati Masters") to bring his career total to 59 top-level singles titles and a record 17 ATP Masters Series titles, having already won seven of the nine ATP Masters tournament—all except the tournaments in [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo_Masters "Monte Carlo Masters") and [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg_Masters "Hamburg Masters"). At 34, he became the second-oldest singles champion in Cincinnati tournament history (the tournament began in 1899), tied with Roger Federer and surpassed only by [Ken Rosewall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Rosewall "Ken Rosewall"), who won the title in 1970 at age 35. He finished the year ranked No. 8, one of the oldest players to finish in the top 10 since the 36-year-old Connors was No. 7 in 1988.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-atpbio-56) At the time, Agassi also became the sixth male player during the [open era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_open_era "Tennis open era") to reach 800 career wins with his first-round victory over [Alex Bogomolov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Bogomolov "Alex Bogomolov") in [Countrywide Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countrywide_Classic "Countrywide Classic") in Los Angeles.

Agassi's 2005 began with a quarterfinal loss to Federer at the Australian Open. Agassi had several other deep runs at tournaments, but had to withdraw from several events due to injury. He lost to [Jarkko Nieminen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarkko_Nieminen "Jarkko Nieminen") in the first round of the French Open. He won his fourth title in Los Angeles and reached the final of the [Rogers Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Masters "Canada Masters"), before falling to No. 2 [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal").

Agassi's 2005 was defined by an improbable run to the US Open final. After beating [Răzvan Sabău](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C4%83zvan_Sab%C4%83u "Răzvan Sabău") and [Ivo Karlović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivo_Karlovi%C4%87 "Ivo Karlović") in straight sets and [Tomáš Berdych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1_Berdych "Tomáš Berdych") in four sets, Agassi won three consecutive five-set matches to advance to the final. The most notable of these matches was his quarterfinal victory over [James Blake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Blake_\(tennis\) "James Blake (tennis)"), where he rallied from two sets down to win in the fifth set tie-breaker. His other five-set victories were over [Xavier Malisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Malisse "Xavier Malisse") in the fourth round and [Robby Ginepri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robby_Ginepri "Robby Ginepri") in the semi-finals. In the final, Agassi faced Federer, who was seeking his second consecutive US Open title and his sixth Grand Slam title in two years. Federer defeated Agassi in four sets. Agassi finished 2005 ranked No. 7, his 16th time in the year-end top-10 rankings, which tied Connors for the most times ranked in the top 10 at year's end.

Agassi had a poor start to 2006, as he was still recovering from an ankle injury and also suffering from back and leg pain and lack of match play. Agassi withdrew from the Australian Open because of the ankle injury, and his back injury and other pains forced him to withdraw from several other events, eventually skipping the entire clay-court season including the French Open. This caused his ranking to drop out of the top 10 for the last time. Agassi returned for the grass-court season, playing a tune-up, and then [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_singles "2006 Wimbledon Championships – Men's singles"). He was defeated in the third round by world No. 2 (and eventual runner-up) [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal"). Against conventions, Agassi, the losing player, was interviewed on court after the match.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-69) At Wimbledon, Agassi announced his plans to retire following the US Open. Agassi played only two events during the summer hard-court season with his best result being a quarterfinal loss at the [Countrywide Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countrywide_Classic "Countrywide Classic") in Los Angeles to [Fernando González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Gonz%C3%A1lez "Fernando González") of Chile, which resulted in him being unseeded at the US Open.

Agassi had a short, but dramatic, run in his final US Open. Because of extreme back pain, Agassi was forced to receive anti-inflammatory injections after every match. After a four-set win against [Andrei Pavel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrei_Pavel "Andrei Pavel"), Agassi faced eighth-seeded [Marcos Baghdatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Baghdatis "Marcos Baghdatis") in the second round who had earlier advanced to the [2006 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Australian_Open "2006 Australian Open") final and Wimbledon semi-finals. Agassi won in five sets as the younger Baghdatis succumbed to muscle cramping in the final set.[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-70) In his last match, Agassi fell to 112th-ranked big-serving [Benjamin Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Becker "Benjamin Becker") of Germany in four sets. Agassi received a four-minute standing ovation from the crowd after the match and delivered a retirement speech.[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-71)
