### Agassi vs. Sampras

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=8 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Sampras")]

Main article: [Agassi–Sampras rivalry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agassi%E2%80%93Sampras_rivalry "Agassi–Sampras rivalry")

The rivalry has been called the greatest of the generation of players competing in the 1990s, as [Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras") and Agassi were the most successful players of that decade. They also had very contrasting playing styles, with Sampras being considered the greatest [server](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serve_\(tennis\) "Serve (tennis)") and Agassi the greatest serve returner at the time. Agassi and Sampras met 34 times on the tour level with Agassi trailing 14–20.[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-72)

The [1990 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_U.S._Open_\(tennis\) "1990 U.S. Open (tennis)") was their first meeting in a Grand Slam tournament final. Agassi was favored as he was ranked No. 4 at the time, compared to the No. 12 ranking of Sampras and because Agassi had defeated Sampras in their only previously completed match. Agassi, however, lost the final to Sampras in straight sets. Their next meeting in a Grand Slam was at the [1992 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_French_Open "1992 French Open"), where they met in the quarterfinals. Although Sampras was ranked higher, Agassi came out winning in straight sets. They met again on a Grand Slam level at the quarterfinals of Wimbledon in 1993, where Agassi was the defending champion and Sampras was the newly minted world No. 1. Agassi dug himself out from a two-sets-to-love hole, levelling the match at two sets apiece; however, Sampras prevailed in five sets, and went on to win his first Wimbledon championship.

With both Sampras and Agassi participating, the [US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Davis_Cup_team "USA Davis Cup team") won the Davis Cup in 1995. The year should be considered the peak of the rivalry as together they won three out of four major titles, meeting each other twice in the finals, and were occupying the top two spots in the rankings for the whole year. They met five times during the year, all in the title matches, including the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Australian_Open "1995 Australian Open"), the [Newsweek Champions Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Wells_Masters "Indian Wells Masters") (now Indian Wells), the [Lipton International Players Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipton_International_Players_Championships "Lipton International Players Championships") (now Miami Open), the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Masters "Canada Masters"), and the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_US_Open_\(tennis\) "1995 US Open (tennis)"). Agassi won three of the finals, including the Australian Open; however, Sampras took the US Open title, ending Agassi's 26-match winning streak. After Agassi had taken most of the fall season off, Sampras took over the No. 1 ranking for the end of the season. Agassi admitted this loss, which gave Sampras a 9–8 lead in their head-to-head meetings, took two years for him to get over it mentally.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tennis-buzz1995-52)

In the following three years, while Sampras continued winning Grand Slam titles every season, Agassi slumped in the rankings and struggled in major competitions. The next time Sampras and Agassi met in a Grand Slam final was at Wimbledon in 1999, where Sampras won in straight sets. For both, it was considered a career rejuvenation, as Sampras had suffered a string of disappointments in the previous year while Agassi was regaining his status as a top-ranked player after winning the French Open. Sampras forfeited the No. 1 ranking to Agassi when injury forced him to withdraw from that year's US Open, which Agassi went on to win. They faced each other twice in the season-ending [ATP Tour World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Masters_Cup "Tennis Masters Cup"), with Sampras losing the round-robin match, but winning the final.

In the 2000s, they met three more times on the Grand Slam level offering three memorable contests. In 2000, the top-ranked Agassi defeated No. 3 Sampras in the semi-finals of the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Australian_Open "2000 Australian Open") in five sets, which was an important win for Agassi who had lost 4 of the previous five matches against Sampras. In arguably their most memorable match ever, Sampras defeated Agassi in the [2001 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_U.S._Open_\(tennis\) "2001 U.S. Open (tennis)") quarterfinals in four sets. There were no breaks of serve during the entire match. Reruns of the match are frequently featured on television, especially during US Open rain delays, and the match is considered one of the best in history because of the level of play presented by both players.

Their last meeting was the final of the [2002 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_U.S._Open_\(tennis\) "2002 U.S. Open (tennis)"), which was their third meeting in a US Open final, but the first since 1995. The match was also notable because they had defeated several up-and-coming players en route to the final. Sampras had defeated No. 3 [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas "Tommy Haas") in the fourth round and future No. 1 [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick "Andy Roddick") in the quarterfinals, while Agassi had defeated No. 1 and defending champion [Lleyton Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lleyton_Hewitt "Lleyton Hewitt") in the semi-finals. Sampras defeated Agassi in four sets. This was the final [ATP tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_tour "ATP tour") singles match of Sampras's career.[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-73)

### Agassi vs. Chang

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=9 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Chang")]

[Michael Chang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Chang "Michael Chang") was the opponent Agassi faced most frequently from all the players other than Sampras. They met 22 times on the tour level with Agassi leading 15–7. Chang, unlike most of Agassi's big rivals, had a playing style similar to his. Both players preferred to stay at the baseline with Chang being more defensive-minded. The outcome was that most of their meetings were built on long and entertaining rallies. The rivalry began late in the 1980s with both players being considered the prodigies of the next great generation of American tennis players and both being of foreign descent.

Agassi won the first four matches including a straight-set victory in round 16 of the 1988 US Open and defeating Chang, the defending champion, in the 1990 French Open in a four-set quarterfinal. Arguably their best match took place in the round of 16 of the 1994 US Open. While both players presented high-quality shot-making, the momentum changed from set to set with Agassi eventually prevailing in a five-set victory. It turned out to be the toughest contest on his way to his first US Open title. Their next two Grand Slam meetings came in 1996, with Chang recording easy straight-set victories in the semi-finals of both the Australian Open and the US Open. Years after, Agassi shockingly admitted in his book that he had lost the first of the matches on purpose as he did not want to face Boris Becker, who was awaiting the winner in the final. Agassi won the last four of their matches, with the last being in 2003 at the Miami Open with Chang being clearly past his prime.

### Agassi vs. Becker

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Becker")]

[Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker "Boris Becker") and Agassi played 14 times with Agassi leading 10–4. Becker won their first three matches in 1988 and 1989 before Agassi reversed the rivalry in 1990, and won 10 of their last 11 matches. One contributing factor is that after their third match, Agassi picked up a tell regarding Becker's serve and knew where his serves would be directed.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-74) They first played at Indian Wells in 1988, with Becker prevailing. Their most notable match was the 1989 [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davis_Cup "Davis Cup") semi-final match, which Becker won in five sets after losing the first two in tiebreaks. Agassi, considered a baseliner with a playing style not suiting grass, shocked Becker, a three-time champion, in a five-set quarterfinal at Wimbledon in 1992 on his way to his first Grand Slam title. The intensity of the rivalry peaked in 1995. Becker won that year's Wimbledon semi-final after being down a set and two breaks, to eventually win in four sets. In a highly anticipated rematch in the US Open semi-final, this time it was Agassi who came out victorious in four tight sets. Their final match was played at Hong Kong in 1999, which Agassi won in three sets.

### Agassi vs. Rafter

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Rafter")]

Agassi and [Pat Rafter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Rafter "Pat Rafter") played fifteen times with Agassi leading 10–5. The rivalry has been considered special and delivered memorable encounters, because of the players' contrasting styles of play, with Rafter using traditional serve-&-volley methods against Agassi's variety of return of serves and passing shots as his main weapons. Agassi led 8–2 on hard courts, but Rafter surprisingly won their sole match on clay at the [1999 Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Italian_Open_\(tennis\) "1999 Italian Open (tennis)"). They played four matches at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Championships,_Wimbledon "The Championships, Wimbledon") with both winning two matches each. Agassi won the first two in 1993 and 1999, while Rafter took their 2000 and 2001 encounters, the latter two matches being gruelling 5-setters often being presented on the lists of best matches ever played. Agassi also won both their meetings at the Australian Open, in 1995 and 2001, on his way to the title on both occasions. Rafter, however, took their only US Open encounter in 1997 and went on to win the title.

### Agassi vs. Federer

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Federer")]

Agassi and [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") played 11 times, and Federer led their head-to-head series 8–3. With the retirement of Sampras, the rivalry against the 11-years-younger Federer, who was another great server like Sampras, became Agassi's main rivalry for the final years of his career. Agassi won their first three matches, but then went on to lose eight consecutive ones. They first met in just the third tournament of Federer's career at the 1998 Swiss Indoors in Federer's hometown, with Agassi prevailing over the 17-year-old. Agassi also defeated Federer at the [2001 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_US_Open_\(tennis\) "2001 US Open (tennis)") and the finals of the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Open_\(tennis\) "Miami Open (tennis)") in 2002. Federer began to turn the tide at the Masters Cup in 2003, when he defeated Agassi in both the round-robin and the final. They played a quarterfinal match at the [2004 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_US_Open_\(tennis\) "2004 US Open (tennis)") that spanned over two windy days, with Federer eventually prevailing in five sets. At the 2005 Dubai Championships, Federer and Agassi attracted worldwide headlines with a publicity stunt that saw the two tennis legends play on a helipad almost 220 meters above sea level at the hotel Burj al-Arab. Their final duel took place in the final of the [2005 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_US_Open_\(tennis\) "2005 US Open (tennis)"). Federer was victorious in four sets in front of a pro-Agassi crowd. The match was the last appearance by Agassi in a Grand Slam final.

### Agassi vs. Lendl

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Lendl")]

Agassi and [Ivan Lendl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Lendl "Ivan Lendl") played eight times, and Lendl led their head-to-head series 6–2.[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-75)

### Agassi vs. Edberg

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Agassi vs. Edberg")]

Agassi and [Stefan Edberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Edberg "Stefan Edberg") played nine times, and Agassi led their head-to-head series 6–3.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-76)

## Post-retirement: Exhibition appearances

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Post-retirement: Exhibition appearances")]

Since retiring after the [2006 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_US_Open_\(tennis\) "2006 US Open (tennis)"), Agassi has participated in a series of charity tournaments and continues his work with his own charity. On September 5, 2007, he was a surprise guest commentator for the [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick "Andy Roddick")/[Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_US_Open_\(tennis\) "2007 US Open (tennis)") quarterfinal. He played an exhibition match at Wimbledon, teaming with his wife, Steffi Graf, to play with [Tim Henman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Henman "Tim Henman") and [Kim Clijsters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Clijsters "Kim Clijsters"). He played [World Team Tennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Team_Tennis "World Team Tennis") for the [Philadelphia Freedoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Freedoms "Philadelphia Freedoms") in the summer of 2009.[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-77) At the [2009 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_French_Open "2009 French Open"), Agassi was on hand to present Roger Federer, who completed his Career Grand Slam by winning the tournament and joined Agassi as one of six men to complete the Career Grand Slam, with the trophy.[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-78)

Also in 2009, Agassi played at the [Outback Champions Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outback_Champions_Series "Outback Champions Series") event for the first time. He played the [Cancer Treatment Centers of America Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cancer_Treatment_Centers_of_America_Championship_at_Surprise "Cancer Treatment Centers of America Championship at Surprise") at [Surprise, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surprise,_Arizona "Surprise, Arizona"), where he reached the final before bowing to eventual champion [Todd Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todd_Martin "Todd Martin").[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-79) Agassi returned to the tour renamed for the PowerShares Series in 2011 and participated in a total of seven events while winning two. Agassi beat Courier in the final of the Staples Champions Cup in [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston "Boston")[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-80) and later defeated Sampras at the CTCA Championships at his hometown Las Vegas.[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-81)

In 2012, Agassi took part in five tournaments, winning three of those. In November, at first he won BILT Champions Showdown in [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California "San Jose, California"), beating [John McEnroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_McEnroe "John McEnroe") in the final.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-82) The following day, he defended his title of the CTCA Championships, while defeating Courier in the decisive match.[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-83) In the series season finale, he beat [Michael Chang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Chang "Michael Chang") for the Acura Champions Cup.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-84) The series and Agassi came back to action in 2014. Agassi won both tournaments he participated in. At the Camden Wealth Advisors Cup's final in Houston, Agassi beat [James Blake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Blake_\(tennis\) "James Blake (tennis)") for a rematch of their 2005 US Open quarterfinal.[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-85) He defeated Blake again in [Portland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portland,_Oregon "Portland, Oregon") to win the title of the Cancer Treatment Centers of America Championships.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-86) In 2015, Agassi took part in just one event of the PowerShares Series, losing to [Mark Philippoussis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Philippoussis "Mark Philippoussis") in the final of the Champions Shootout.[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-87) The following year he took part in two events, at first losing to Blake in Chicago, and the next day defeating Mardy Fish, but losing to Roddick in Charleston.[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-88)

In 2009, in [Macau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macau "Macau") Agassi and Sampras met for the first time on court since the 2002 US Open final. Sampras won the exhibition in three sets.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-89) The rivalry between the former champions headlined sports media again in March 2010 after the two participated in the "Hit for Haiti" charity event organized to raise money for the victims of the [earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake "2010 Haiti earthquake"). Partnered with Roger Federer and [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal"), the old rivals began making jokes at each other's expense, which ended up with Sampras intentionally striking a serve at Agassi's body. After the event, Agassi admitted that he had crossed the line with his jokes and publicly apologized to Sampras.[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-90) Agassi and Sampras met again one year later for an exhibition match at [Madison Square Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_Square_Garden "Madison Square Garden") in New York in front of 19 000 spectators as Sampras defeated Agassi in two sets.[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-91) On March 3, 2014, Agassi and Sampras squared off for an exhibition in London for the annual [World Tennis Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Tennis_Day "World Tennis Day"). This time, it was Agassi who came out on top in two straight sets.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-92)

He returned to the tour in May 2017 in the position of coach to [Novak Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic") for the French Open.[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-93) Agassi announced the end of the partnership on March 31, 2018, stating that there were too many disagreements in the relationship.[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-94)

## Legacy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Legacy")]

Considered by numerous sources to be one of the greatest tennis players of all time,[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SI-12)[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tennis-15)[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cba-95)[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-96)[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-stars-97) Agassi has also been called one of the greatest service returners ever to play the game, and was described by the [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC "BBC") upon his retirement as "perhaps the biggest worldwide star in the sport's history".[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cba-95)[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-stars-97)[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-98)[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-99)[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-100) As a result, he is credited for helping to revive the popularity of tennis during the 1990s.[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cba-95)[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-stars-97)[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-101)

Among his numerous career accolades, Agassi was named the [BBC Overseas Sports Personality of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_Overseas_Sports_Personality_of_the_Year "BBC Overseas Sports Personality of the Year") in 1992, and the 7th greatest male player of all time by *[Sports Illustrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Illustrated "Sports Illustrated")* in 2010.[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-SI-12) On July 9, 2011, Agassi was inducted into the [International Tennis Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Tennis_Hall_of_Fame "International Tennis Hall of Fame") at a ceremony in Newport, Rhode Island.[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tennis-15)

Agassi earned more than $30 million in prize-money during his career, sixth only to Djokovic, Federer, Nadal, Sampras and Murray to date (May 2018). He also earned more than $25 million a year through endorsements during his career, which was ranked fourth in all sports at the time.[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-102)

## Playing style

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Playing style")]

Early in his career, Agassi would look to end points quickly by playing first-strike tennis, typically by inducing a weak return with a deep, hard shot, and then playing a winner at an extreme angle. On the rare occasion that he charged the net, Agassi liked to take the ball in the air and hit a swinging volley for a winner. His favored groundstroke was his flat, accurate two-handed backhand, hit well cross-court but especially down the line. His forehand was nearly as strong, especially his inside-out to the ad court.[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-103)

Agassi's strength was in dictating play from the baseline, and he was able to consistently take the ball on the rise.[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-104) While he was growing up, his father and Nick Bollettieri trained him in this way.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-105) When in control of a point, Agassi would often pass up an opportunity to attempt a winner and hit a conservative shot to minimize his errors, and to make his opponent run more. This change to more methodical, less aggressive baseline play was largely initiated by his longtime coach, Brad Gilbert, in their first year together in 1994. Gilbert encouraged Agassi to wear out opponents with his deep, flat groundstrokes and to use his fitness to win attrition wars, and noted Agassi's two-handed backhand down the line as his very best shot.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-105) A signature play later in his career was a change-up drop shot to the deuce court after deep penetrating groundstrokes. This would often be followed by a passing shot or lob if the opponent was fast enough to retrieve it.

Agassi was raised on hardcourts, but found much of his early major-tournament success on the red clay of Roland Garros, reaching two consecutive finals there early in his career. Despite grass being his worst surface, his first major win was at the slick grass of Wimbledon in 1992, a tournament that he professed to hating at the time.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-105) His strongest surface over the course of his career, was indeed hardcourt, where he won six of his eight majors.

## Business ventures

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Business ventures")]

Agassi established a [limited liability company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limited_liability_company "Limited liability company") named Andre Agassi Ventures (formerly named Agassi Enterprises).[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-106) Agassi, along with five athlete partners (including [Wayne Gretzky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_Gretzky "Wayne Gretzky"), [Joe Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Montana "Joe Montana"), [Shaquille O'Neal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaquille_O%27Neal "Shaquille O'Neal"), [Ken Griffey Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Griffey_Jr. "Ken Griffey Jr."), and [Monica Seles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_Seles "Monica Seles")) opened a chain of sports-themed restaurant named [Official All Star Café](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_All_Star_Caf%C3%A9 "Official All Star Café") in April 1996. The restaurant closed down in 2001.[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-107)

In 1999, he paid $1 million for a 10 percent stake in Nevada First Bank and made a $10 million profit when it was sold to Western Alliance Bancorp in 2006.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108)

In 2002, he joined the [Tennis Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_Channel "Tennis Channel") to promote the channel to consumers and cable and satellite industry, and made an equity investment in the network.[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-109) After meeting chef [Michael Mina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Mina "Michael Mina") at one of his restaurants in San Francisco, Agassi partnered with him in 2002 to start Mina Group Inc. and opened 18 concept restaurants in San Francisco, [San Jose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jose,_California "San Jose, California"), [Dana Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Point "Dana Point"), [Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City "Atlantic City") and Las Vegas.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-110)[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-111) Agassi was an equity investor of a group that acquired [Golden Nugget Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Nugget_Las_Vegas "Golden Nugget Las Vegas") and [Golden Nugget Laughlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Nugget_Laughlin "Golden Nugget Laughlin") from [MGM Mirage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirage_Resorts "Mirage Resorts") for $215 million in 2004.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108)[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-112) One year later, the group sold the hotel-casino to [Landry's, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landry%27s,_Inc. "Landry's, Inc.") for $163 million in cash and $182 million in assumed debt.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108)[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-113) In 2007, he sat on the board of Meadows Bank, an independent bank in Nevada.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-114) He has invested in start-up companies backed by [Allen & Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allen_%26_Company "Allen & Company").[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-115)

Agassi and Graf formed a company called Agassi Graf Holdings. They invested in PURE, a nightclub at [Caesars Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesars_Palace "Caesars Palace"), which opened in 2004,[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108) and sold it to Angel Management Group in 2010.[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-116) In August 2006, Agassi and Graf developed a joint venture with high-end furniture maker [Kreiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kreiss "Kreiss") Enterprises.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108) They launched a furniture line called Agassi Graf Collection.[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-117)[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-118) In September, Agassi and Graf, through their company Agassi Graf Development LLC, along with Bayview Financial LP, finalized an agreement to develop a condominium hotel, Fairmont Tamarack, at [Tamarack Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamarack_Resort "Tamarack Resort") in [Donnelly, Idaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donnelly,_Idaho "Donnelly, Idaho").[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bloomberg2007-108)[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-119) Owing to difficult market conditions and delays, they withdrew from the project in 2009.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-120)[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-121) The group still owns three small chunks of land.[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-122) In September, they collaborated with [Steve Case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Case "Steve Case")'s Exclusive Resorts to co-develop luxury resorts and design Agassi-Graf Tennis and Fitness Centers.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-123)

They also invested in online ticket reseller [viagogo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viagogo "Viagogo") in 2009 and both serve as board members and advisors of the company.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-124)[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-125)

In October 2012, [Village Roadshow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Village_Roadshow "Village Roadshow") and investors including Agassi and Graf announced plans to build a new water park called [Wet'n'Wild Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet%27n%27Wild_Las_Vegas "Wet'n'Wild Las Vegas") in Las Vegas. Village Roadshow has a 51% stake in the park while Agassi, Graf, and other private investors hold the remaining 49%.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-126)[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-127) The park opened in May 2013.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)

[IMG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMG_\(company\) "IMG (company)") managed Agassi from the time he turned pro in 1986 through January 2000 before switching to SFX Sports Group.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129)[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-130) His business manager, lawyer and agent was childhood friend Perry Rogers, but they have been estranged since 2008.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-131)[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-132) In 2009, he and Graf signed with [CAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Artists_Agency "Creative Artists Agency").[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-133)

### Equipment and endorsements

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Equipment and endorsements")]

Agassi used [Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Sports "Prince Sports") [Graphite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_original_graphite "Prince original graphite") rackets early in his career.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-134)[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-135) He signed a $7 million endorsement contract with Belgian tennis racquet makers [Donnay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donnay_\(sports\) "Donnay (sports)").[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-LATimes1990-136) He later switched to [Head Ti Radical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_\(company\) "Head (company)") racket[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-137) and Head's LiquidMetal Radical racket, having signed a multimillion-dollar endorsement deal with Head in 1993.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-head2003-138)[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-139) He renewed his contract in 1999, and in November 2003 he signed a lifetime agreement with Head.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-head2003-138)[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-forbes2004-140) He also endorses [Penn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Racquet_Sports "Penn Racquet Sports") tennis balls. On July 25, 2005, Agassi left [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc. "Nike, Inc.") after 17 years and signed an endorsement deal with [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas "Adidas").[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-141) A major reason for Agassi leaving Nike was because Nike refused to donate to Agassi's charities, and Adidas did. On May 13, 2013, Agassi rejoined Nike.[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-142)[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-143)[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-144)

Agassi was sponsored by [DuPont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuPont_\(1802%E2%80%932017\) "DuPont (1802–2017)"),[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-145)\[*[non-primary source needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:No_original_research#Primary,_secondary_and_tertiary_sources "Wikipedia:No original research")*][\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-146) [Ebel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebel "Ebel"),[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-LATimes1990-136) [Mountain Dew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Dew "Mountain Dew") in 1993,[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-147) [Mazda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazda "Mazda") in 1997,[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-kiamotors-148) [Kia Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kia_Motors "Kia Motors") in 2002,[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-forbes2004-140)[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-kiamotors-148) [American Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Express "American Express")[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-149) and [Deutsche Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Bank "Deutsche Bank") in 2003.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-150) In 1990, he appeared in a television commercial for [Canon Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_Inc. "Canon Inc."), promoting the [Canon EOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_EOS "Canon EOS") Rebel camera.[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-canon2004-151) Between 1999 and 2000, he signed a multimillion-dollar, multiyear endorsement deal with [Schick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schick_\(razors\) "Schick (razors)") and became the worldwide spokesman for the company.[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-152) Agassi signed a multiyear contract with [Twinlab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twinlab "Twinlab") and promoted the company's nutritional supplements.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-153) In mid-2003, he was named the spokesman of Aramis Life, a fragrance by [Aramis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Est%C3%A9e_Lauder_Companies "Estée Lauder Companies"), and signed a five-year deal with the company.[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-154)[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-155) In March 2004, he signed a ten-year agreement worth $1.5 million a year with [24 Hour Fitness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24_Hour_Fitness "24 Hour Fitness"), which will open five Andre Agassi fitness centers by year-end.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-forbes2004-140) Prior to the 2012 Australian Open, Agassi and Australian winemaker [Jacobs Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobs_Creek_\(Australia\) "Jacobs Creek (Australia)") announced a three-year partnership and created the Open Film Series to "\[share] personal stories about the life defining moments that shaped his character on and off the court."[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-156) In 2007, watchmaker [Longines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longines "Longines") named Agassi as their brand ambassador.[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-157)[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-158)

Agassi and his mother appeared in a [Got Milk?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Got_Milk%3F "Got Milk?") advertisement in 2002.

Agassi has appeared in many advertisements and television commercials with Graf. They both endorsed [Deutsche Telekom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Telekom "Deutsche Telekom") in 2002,[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-159) [Genworth Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genworth_Financial "Genworth Financial")[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-genworth-160) and [Canon Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_Inc. "Canon Inc.")[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-canon2004-151) in 2004, [LVMH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LVMH "LVMH") in 2007,[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-161) and [Nintendo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo "Nintendo") [Wii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wii "Wii")[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-162) and [Wii Fit U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wii_Fit_U "Wii Fit U")[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-163) and [Longines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longines "Longines") in 2013.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-164)

## In popular culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: In popular culture")]

In 2017, Agassi appeared in the documentary film *[Love Means Zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Means_Zero "Love Means Zero")*, which highlighted the troubled relationship between his coach [Nick Bollettieri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Bollettieri "Nick Bollettieri") and him.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165)[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166)

## Other endeavors

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Other endeavors")]

### Politics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Politics")]

Agassi has donated more than $100,000 to Democratic candidates, and $2,000 to Republicans.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167)[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-168) On September 1, 2010, when he appeared on daily [WNYC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNYC "WNYC") public radio program *[The Brian Lehrer Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Brian_Lehrer_Show "The Brian Lehrer Show")*, he stated that he is registered as Independent.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-169)

### Philanthropy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Philanthropy")]

Agassi founded the [Andre Agassi Charitable Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Agassi_Charitable_Association "Andre Agassi Charitable Association") in 1994, which assists Las Vegas' young people. He was awarded the [ATP Arthur Ashe Humanitarian award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Arthur_Ashe_Humanitarian_award "ATP Arthur Ashe Humanitarian award") in 1995 for his efforts to help disadvantaged youth. He has been cited as the most charitable and socially involved player in professional tennis. It has also been claimed that he may be the most charitable athlete of his generation.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-170)

Agassi's charities help in assisting children reach their athletic potential. His Boys & Girls Club sees 2,000 children throughout the year and boasts a world-class junior tennis team. It also has a basketball program (the Agassi Stars) and a rigorous system that encourages a mix of academics and athletics.

In 2001, Agassi opened the Andre Agassi College Preparatory Academy[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Prep-171) in Las Vegas, a tuition-free charter school for at-risk children in the area. He personally donated $35 million to the school.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-172) In 2009, the graduating class had a 100 percent graduation rate and expected a 100 percent college acceptance rate.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-173) Among other child-related programs that Agassi supports through his Andre Agassi Charitable Foundation is Clark County's only residential facility for abused and neglected children, Child Haven. In 1997, Agassi donated funding to Child Haven for a six-room classroom building now named the Agassi Center for Education. His foundation also provided $720,000 to assist in the building of the Andre Agassi Cottage for Medically Fragile Children. This 20-bed facility opened in December 2001, and accommodates developmentally delayed or handicapped children and children quarantined for infectious diseases.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174)

In 2007, along with several other athletes, Agassi founded the charity [Athletes for Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletes_for_Hope "Athletes for Hope"), which helps professional athletes get involved in charitable causes and aims to inspire all people to volunteer and support their communities.[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175) He created the Canyon-Agassi Charter School Facilities Fund, now known as the Turner-Agassi Charter School Facilities Fund. The Fund is an investment initiative for social change, focusing on the "nationwide effort to move charters from stopgap buildings into permanent campuses."[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176)

In September 2013, the Andre Agassi Foundation for Education formed a partnership with V20 Foods to launch Box Budd!es, a line of kids' healthy snacks. All proceeds go to the Foundation.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177)[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178)

In February 2014, Agassi remodeled the vacant [University of Phoenix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Phoenix "University of Phoenix") building in Las Vegas as a new school, called the Doral Academy West through the Canyon-Agassi Charter School Facilities Fund. Doral Academy opened in August 2014.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-179) The Fund purchased a 4.6-acre plot in [Henderson, Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henderson,_Nevada "Henderson, Nevada"), to house the Somerset Academy of Las Vegas, which will relocate from its campus inside a church.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)

On April 2, 2023, Agassi participated with Michael Chang, Andy Roddick and John McEnroe in the first live airing of Pickleball on ESPN in the Million dollar Pickleball Slam at the Hard Rock Casino in Hollywood, Fla.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181)

## Personal life

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Personal life")]

### Relationships and family

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Relationships and family")]

In the early 1990s, after dating Wendi Stewart,[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182) Agassi dated American singer and entertainer [Barbra Streisand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbra_Streisand "Barbra Streisand"). He wrote about the relationship in his 2009 autobiography, "We agree that we're good for each other, and so what if she's twenty-eight years older? We're sympatico, and the public outcry only adds spice to our connection. It makes our friendship feel forbidden, taboo — another piece of my overall rebellion. Dating Barbra Streisand is like wearing Hot Lava."[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183)

He was married to actress and socialite [Brooke Shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_Shields "Brooke Shields") from 1997 to 1999.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184)

He married [Steffi Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steffi_Graf "Steffi Graf") on October 22, 2001, at their [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas "Las Vegas") home; the only witnesses were their mothers.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185) They have two children: son Jaden Gil (born 2001) and daughter Jaz Elle (born 2003).[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-186) Agassi has said that he and Graf are not pushing their children toward becoming tennis players.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187) The Graf-Agassi family resides in [Summerlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summerlin,_Nevada "Summerlin, Nevada"), a community in the [Las Vegas Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Valley "Las Vegas Valley").[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-188) Graf's mother and brother, Michael, with his four children, also live there.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-189)

His mother is a breast cancer survivor.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-190)

Long-time trainer [Gil Reyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gil_Reyes_\(tennis\) "Gil Reyes (tennis)") has been called one of Agassi's closest friends; some have described him as being a "father figure" to Agassi.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191)[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192) In 2012, Agassi and Reyes introduced their own line of fitness equipment, BILT By Agassi and Reyes.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-193) In December 2008, Agassi's childhood friend and former business manager, Perry Rogers, sued Graf for $50,000 in management fees he claimed that she owed him.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-194)[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195)

### Autobiography

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Autobiography")]

Main article: [Open: An Autobiography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open:_An_Autobiography "Open: An Autobiography")

Agassi's autobiography, *[Open: An Autobiography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open:_An_Autobiography "Open: An Autobiography")*, (written with assistance from [J. R. Moehringer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._R._Moehringer "J. R. Moehringer")[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196)), was published in November 2009. In it, Agassi talks about his childhood and his unconventional Armenian father, who came to the United States from Iran, where he was a professional boxer. Overly demanding and emotionally abusive to the whole family, his father groomed young Agassi for tennis greatness by building a tennis court in their backyard and sending Agassi to tennis boarding school under the supervision of Nick Bollettieri, who later coached and managed part of Agassi's professional career.[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197)

There is also mention in the book of using and testing positive for [methamphetamine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methamphetamine "Methamphetamine") in 1997.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-sports.espn.go.com-53)[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-198)[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-199) In response to this revelation, [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") declared himself shocked and disappointed, while [Marat Safin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marat_Safin "Marat Safin") argued that Agassi should return his prize money and be stripped of his titles.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-200)[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-201) In an interview with CBS, Agassi justified himself and asked for understanding, saying that "It was a period in my life where I needed help."[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-202)

Agassi said that he had always hated tennis during his career because of the constant pressure it exerted on him. He also said he wore a hairpiece earlier in his career and thought [Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras") was "robotic".[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203)

The book reached No. 1 on the [*New York Times* Best Seller list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Times_Best_Seller_list "New York Times Best Seller list")[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204) and received favorable reviews.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205) It won the Autobiography category of the 2010 [British Sports Book Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Sports_Book_Awards "British Sports Book Awards").[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) In 2018, the book was listed on *[Esquire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esquire_\(magazine\) "Esquire (magazine)")* as one of "The 30 Best Sports Books Ever Written",[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207) and was also recommended by self-help author [Tim Ferriss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Ferriss "Tim Ferriss") who described it as "very candid, very amusing, and very instructional".[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208)

### Grand Slam finals (8 titles, 7 runner-ups)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Grand Slam finals (8 titles, 7 runner-ups)")]

By winning the [1999 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_French_Open "1999 French Open"), Agassi completed a men's singles Career Grand Slam. He is the 5th of 8 male players in history (after [Budge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Budge "Don Budge"), [Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Perry "Fred Perry"), [Laver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver "Rod Laver") and [Emerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Emerson "Roy Emerson"), and before [Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer"), [Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal") and [Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic")) to achieve this.

## Notes

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Andre_Agassi\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Notes")]

1. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-10)** Career Golden Slam also achieved by [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal") and [Novak Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic").
2. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-11)** Career Super Slam also achieved by [Novak Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic").
3. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-58)** [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") has since surpassed this feat, reaching ten consecutive Grand Slam finals from 2005 to 2007.
4. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-59)** [Pete Sampras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sampras "Pete Sampras") held the 1993 Wimbledon, [1993 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_US_Open_\(tennis\) "1993 US Open (tennis)") and [1994 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Australian_Open "1994 Australian Open") titles simultaneously. [Jimmy Connors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Connors "Jimmy Connors") (1974), [Roger Federer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Federer "Roger Federer") (2004, 2006 and 2007) and [Novak Djokovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novak_Djokovic "Novak Djokovic") (2011) won those three majors in the same year, although Connors' Grand Slam titles were all played on grass courts. [Mats Wilander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mats_Wilander "Mats Wilander") won all but Wimbledon in 1988 during his similar rise to the year-end No. 1. [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal "Rafael Nadal") won the French Open and Wimbledon "Channel Slam" (2008) and 2009 Australian Open, before replicating the Channel Slam alongside winning the US Open in 2010.
5. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-209)** Abbreviation for "[Year-end championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_World_Tour_Finals "ATP World Tour Finals")".
6. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-210)** A "Career Super Slam" entails winning all four [majors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Slam_\(tennis\) "Grand Slam (tennis)"), the [Year-End Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_World_Tour_Finals "ATP World Tour Finals") and the [Olympic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_Summer_Olympics "Tennis at the Summer Olympics") gold medal in singles.
